# Roll-call & self-identification evidence, 2023–2024 Claremont / SAU 6 transcripts

**Scope:** every `.json` in `Input/Transcripts` with show ID 14875–16226 (65 files), plus the
title-only file `Claremont School Board Meeting 2123.mp4.json` = **66 meetings**, ordered by
meeting date decoded from the filename.

**What this document is.** Raw ASR evidence harvested mechanically, so a roster can be built
*from* it. Everything inside a fenced block is quoted **verbatim as the ASR heard it**, with the
segment index in `[n]` (opening block) or `<n>` (pattern hits) and the diarized label the
transcript assigned. **Nothing in the quoted blocks has been corrected.** The commentary below
distinguishes what the roll calls actually evidence from what is only suggestive.

**How it was produced.** One script walked all 66 files, joined each segment's `words[].text`
with spaces (empty strings stripped), and emitted per meeting: segments 0–39 verbatim; every
segment anywhere in the file matching a self-identification pattern (`my name is`, `I'm the`,
`I am the`, `Ward one/two/three/1/2/3`, `, Ward`, `state representative`); every segment in
0–79 matching an address pattern (`Mr.` `Mrs.` `Ms.` `Miss` `Dr.` `Doctor` `Superintendent`
`Principal` `Chairman` `Madam` `Chair X` `Thank you, X`); plus segment and label counts.

**Two bodies.** `SchoolBoard*` files are the **Claremont School Board**. `SAU6*` files are the
**SAU 6 joint board** — a different body, seating Claremont members *and* Unity members
together, which is why its roll calls are roughly twice as long.

---

## A. Chronological roster as the roll calls alone support it

### Era 1 — through the March 14, 2023 town election

Names actually spoken in a roll call or a formal introduction in this window:

| Heard as | Meeting / segment |
|---|---|
| `Michael patron` | 3/1/23 `[10]` |
| `Heather. Whitney` | 3/1/23 `[10]`, SAU6 2/16/23 `<7>` |
| `Bonnie miles` / `Bonnie. Miles` | 3/1/23 `[11]`, SAU6 2/16/23 `<9>` |
| `Frank Sprague` | 3/1/23 `[11]`, SAU6 2/16/23 `<7>` |
| `Whitney. Skillet. Here.` / `Skilling here` | 3/1/23 `[12]`, 2/1/23 `[14]` |
| `Steve Gorski here` | SAU6 2/16/23 `<7>` |
| `Greg` (`Thanks. Greg.`) | 2/1/23 `[11]` |

The 2/8/2023 deliberative session gives the only clean, formal introduction of the pre-election
board (14892 `[3]`–`[4]`):

> `Now, as chairman of the school board, Michael Patron to introduce the school board and the
> school district officers.` … `We have Frank Sprague, school board member Stephen Hauschka,
> school board member, Whitney School and school board member, Bonnie Miles, board member. And
> Heather Whitney, vice chair.`

**Evidenced:** Michael Patron[e] chairs; Heather Whitney is vice chair; Sprague, Hauschka,
"Whitney School"[=Skilling], Miles are members. **Suggestive only:** `Greg` in the 2/1/23 roll
call is almost certainly Sprague — later roll calls render him `Greg Sprague` (9/6/23 `[4]`),
`Craig Sprague` (3/6/24 `[2]`), `Frank Spriggs` (12/18/24 `[1]`), `Thanks, Bragg` (SAU6 4/11/24
`[34]`) — but the 2/1/23 segment alone does not prove it.

### The March 2023 election

2/15/23 `<478>`: `three people running for the three year seat is Frank Sprague, Nicholas Stone,
and Arlene Hopkins … And two people running` — and 3/1/23 `<266>`–`<267>` from the chair:
`there are three people running for the … three year seat, Mr. Frank Sprague. Mr. Nicholas Stone
and Miss Arlene Hawkins … and then for the one year seat is, Jennifer Gallagher, who was just
here, and myself`.

**Evidenced by the next roll calls:** on 4/5/23 and 4/19/23 the names read are Whitney, Sprague,
Miles, Skilling, Hauschka, **Gallagher**, **Hawkins** — and `Michael patron` is gone. So Sprague
and Hawkins took the two three-year seats and Gallagher the one-year seat; Stone and the sitting
chair did not return. (The transcripts never announce vote totals; this is inference from the
roll calls.)

### Era 2 — April 5, 2023 to June 21, 2023 (7 members)

4/5/23 is the organizational meeting (15003 `[2]`–`[17]`): `I'd be pleased to nominate Heather
Whitney as chair of the Claremont School Board` … `All in favor of having Frank Sprague be the
vice chair of the Claremont School Board`. Roll `[23]`–`[24]`: `Heather Whitney here. Stephen
Hausman here. Bonnie miles here. Frank Sprague here.` / `You still here? Jennifer Gallagher
present.`

4/19/23 `[9]` is the cleanest full roll of this era: `Arlene Hawkins. Yes. Bonnie. Miles. Yes.
Whitney. Skilling. Yes. Jennifer Gallagher. Yes. Thanks. Sprague. Yes.` and `[26]`–`[31]` adds
`Steven. Here.` / `Frank Sprague present. Heather Whitney. And everybody is here.`

**Chair: Heather Whitney. Vice chair: Frank Sprague. Both hold those posts continuously through
the end of 2024** (re-elected 3/20/24 `[3]`–`[16]`; still introduced that way 11/19/24 `[0]`).

### Era 3 — the June 21, 2023 resignation

15153 `[20]`–`[22]`, spoken by a member the chair then calls `Steve` (`[26]`):

> `Chair Whitney and vice chair Sprague. Tonight, I am disheartened to be resigning from my
> position as a member of the Claremont School Board, effective immediately.` … `a lack of
> respect and personal attacks from board members, especially when they interfere with my
> ability to do my job as a board member and saw six chair.`

**Evidenced:** the member heard as Hauschka/Hausman/Gorski/Horse resigns 6/21/2023 and was
simultaneously SAU 6 board chair. Board runs six-strong for four weeks.

### Era 4 — July 19, 2023: the appointment

15205 shows three candidates interviewed for the vacancy — `My name is David Bailey. I'm homeowner
here in Claremont` `<191>`; `Kevin Tyson, Ward two` `<240>`; and Candace Crawford — then `<293>`:
`that is our 4 to 2 vote. We. Miss Crawford is have been appointed to the school. Welcome.`

**Era 4 board (July 2023 – March 2024):** Whitney (chair), Sprague (vice chair), Miles, Skilling,
Gallagher, Hawkins, Crawford. Typical roll, 9/6/23 `[4]`–`[5]`: `Heather Whitney, Greg Sprague
here.` / `And Bonnie Myles here with me still here. Jennifer Gallagher and is Crawford here?
Arlene Hawkins here.`

The 2/3/2024 deliberative session confirms the whole set with committee assignments (15552
`<9>`–`<11>`): Heather Whitney chair; Frank Sprague vice chair and Finance chair; Candace
Crawford; Jen Gallagher; Bonnie Miles (absent, Capital chair); Arlene Hawkins (SAU 6 board
chair); Whitney Skilling (Policy chair).

### Era 5 — the March 2024 election

3/6/24 `<623>`: `since Miss Gallagher is not going to be on the school board after the election`.
3/20/24 is the reorganization meeting: Whitney re-elected chair `[3]`–`[11]`, Sprague vice chair
`[15]`–`[17]`, then `[24]`: `I would like to welcome reelected or new board members as you were
appointed. Miss Crawford and Michael Patron back to the board.`

**Era 5 board (March 2024 – end of 2024):** Whitney (chair), Sprague (vice chair), Miles,
Skilling, Hawkins, Crawford, **Patron[e] (returning)**. Clean roll, 4/17/24 `[3]`: `Candace.
Crawfords, here are Hawkins here. Bonnie Myers here. Michael. Patron here. Whitney. Stealin
here. Frank Sprague here. Heather Whitney here.`

- 3/30/2023 organizational meeting (14986 `[10]`–`[44]`): nominations `Marjorie Erickson` and

## B. Superintendent, business administrator, and the January 2024 rupture

**Michael Tempesta — superintendent, start of the window to 1/12/2024.** Named constantly
(`Mr. Tempesta`, `Mike Tempesta`, and garbles `Mr. Tempest`, `Mr. Pesto`, `Mr. Contessa`,
`Mr. Dempster`, `The Tempest`, `Mike pesca`, `Mr. Thompson`). Role stated outright at the
11/30/23 finance meeting (15443 `[1]`).

**The termination — SAU 6, 1/11/2024 (15523 `[25]`, `[34]`, `[40]`):**

> `I'm going to make a motion that, in accordance with paragraph nine of the contract between the
> board and Michael Tempesta, that the Saw board terminate his contract by paying six months
> severance pay and benefits, with the effective date of separation being January 12th, 2024.`
> … `I move that the Saw board appoint Christopher Pratt as interim superintendent of schools
> effective January 12th, 2024`

Roll on the termination `[39]` records one `No` (Marjorie Erickson, `[35]`) and one abstention
(`Shannon … I'm abstaining from this`).

**Christopher Pratt.** Stevens High School principal in 2023 (`Mr. Pratt from principal of
Stevens High School`, 9/6/23 `<100>`; `principal Christopher Pratt`, 11/1/23 `[37]`). Interim
superintendent from 1/12/2024 — welcomed 1/17/24 `<23>`: `welcome Mr. Pratt to his first school
board meeting as interim superintendent … an administrator who has been a superintendent for
eight years`. Named as interim at the 2/3/24 deliberative `<7>`. **Permanent appointment stated
only once, SAU 6 9/12/2024 `<31>`:** `I would like to formally announce the appointment of
Superintendent Christopher Pratt as superintendent of saw six, which was effective in June.`
(Corroborating aside, 8/21/24 `<568>`: `Mr. Pratt was an interim superintendent before you became
the superintendent`.) **So the evidence says interim 1/12/2024, permanent effective June 2024 —
not May.** Salary/contract action 12/12/24 `[27]`–`[32]`: 3% increase, two-year contract.

**Richard Seaman — business administrator to April 2023.** Heard as bare `Richard` on 2/1/23
`<2>`, `<275>`, `<536>` and 2/15/23 `<269>`, `<457>`, and as `Mr. Seaman` at the 2/8/23
deliberative `<47>`, `<50>`. Retirement letter read at SAU 6 3/30/2023 `<123>`: `a letter of
retirement … From Richard Seaman … as I am soon to reach age 65. I will be retiring from my
position effective April 14th`.

**Interregnum.** 5/3/23 `<30>`: `an update on the business administrator search. So far, there
have been three initial interviews`. `Dave Jack` of MRI covered the gap — SAU 6 5/11/23 `<160>`:
`My name is Dave Jack. I'm consultant with MRI municipal resources … when there's a vacancy in
the business office`.

**Mary Henry — business administrator from July 2023.** SAU 6 7/13/23 `<169>`: `It's been great
to have Mary Henry join us and really give some guidance on financials`, and `<186>`: `I don't
have a budget update for you because I've just been here basically … it's only been a few days`.
Title stated flat out 11/30/23 `<1>` and 11/19/24 `[7]` (`Mary Henry, business administrator`).
Her own history, 9/30/24 `<239>`: `I've been in Bay now for about eight years … my background
starts as a school board member at Fall Mountain, starting in 2011 through 2022`.

**The single most useful line in the whole set — 11/30/2023 finance, 15443 `[0]`–`[1]`:**

> `My name is Frank Sprague. I'm the chair of this committee and vice chair of the Claremont
> Board. Heather Whitney is on the committee. She's the chair of the Claremont board. And
> Candace Crawford is also on the committee. Claremont board member and Mike Tempesta,
> superintendent. Mike Kosky, assistant superintendent. Mary Henry, business administrator.
> And Ben Nester, who is our special ed director.`

**Second-most useful — 11/19/2024 finance, 16155 `[0]`–`[9]`** (a full round of self-IDs):
Frank Sprague (vice chair / finance chair), Heather Whitney (chair), Candace Crawford,
`I'm Michelle Harrington, the assistant director at the Tech center`, `Michael Harrington,
principal of Stevens High School`, `Frank Miller, principal, Claremont Middle School` [the same
speaker is addressed as `Mr. Romeo` at `<186>`], `But once a principal at Maple` [= Blunt],
`Mary Henry, business administrator`, `Baxter Hearn, director of human resources`,
`Chelsea Weatherford, executive assistant to the superintendent`.

## D. Recurring garbles — the strongest signal available

These are the same speech rendered differently across many meetings. Recurrence, not any single
rendering, is the evidence.

- **Sprague** → `Frank Sprague` / `Greg Sprague` / `Craig Sprague` / `Frank Spriggs` /
  `Thanks. Sprague` / `Thanks, Bragg` / `Craig` / `Greg` / `upon your great Sprague` /
  `Mr. Spring` / `Mr. Springs`.
- **Skilling** → `Whitney Skilling` / `Skillet` / `Whitney still here` / `Whitney. Stealin` /
  `Ricky Skilling` / `Goody. Skilling` / `Vinnie. Skilling` / `Beneath. Skilling` /
  `with me still here` / `Wendy. Skilling`. **And, critically, `Miss Gillen` / `Miss Gillan` /
  `Miss Gilman` / `Miss Guilherme` / `Miss Kellen` / `Miss Skill` — 3/20/24 `<500>` uses
  `Miss Whitney Scullin` and `Miss Whitney Gillan` for the same person in one paragraph.**
- **Hawkins** → `Arlene Hawkins` / `Arlene Hopkins` / `Carly Hawkins` / `Harley Hawkins` /
  `Marlene. Hawkins` / `Eileen Hawkins` / `Eileen Hopkins` / `Riley Hawkins` / `Arlene Foster` /

## E. What I could not resolve, and warnings for the next stage

1. **`14875 SchoolBoard020123` and `Claremont School Board Meeting 2123` are the same meeting.**
   Both are 2/1/2023: same opening, same roll call, same citizens' comments. Two independent
   ASR + diarization passes (774 segments / 35 labels vs. 825 segments / 34 labels). Treat as one
   meeting with two transcripts — they are a free cross-check on every garble in that meeting,
   e.g. `Skilling here` vs `Skillet. Here.`; `Gomer` vs `mascot`; `Mr. Pratt` vs `Mr. Paterno's`.

2. **`15299 SAU6110923` sorts out of show-ID order.** Its ID sits among September 2023 files but
   the content is the 11/9/2023 SAU 6 meeting. Date the meeting from the filename's date field,
   not from the show ID.

3. **Most roll calls are useless on their own.** In a large share of meetings the ASR captured
   only `Here. Here. Here.` — the names were spoken too quickly or too quietly. Examples: 12/20/23
   `[3]`–`[5]`, 11/20/24 `[4]`–`[5]`, 12/4/24 `[3]`–`[4]`, 10/16/24 `[2]`. For those meetings,
   attendance must come from elsewhere; there is no name evidence in the roll call at all.

4. **`Miss Gillen` is the single highest-risk garble in the corpus.** It occurs dozens of times
   and reads like a distinct person. The 3/20/24 `<500>`–`<501>` passage is the proof that it is
   Whitney Skilling, and it should be checked before any attribution pass runs.

5. **Two different Harringtons overlap in 2024** — Dr. Michael Harrington (Stevens High School)
   and Michelle Harrington (tech center). Between September and December 2024 both are in the
   room and both are called `Mr./Miss Harrington`, sometimes in the same meeting (11/19/24).

6. **`Mr. Kosky` vs. the Hauschka/Gorski family of garbles.** At 2/1/23 (duplicate transcript) `<380>` and
   2/8/23 `<154>` the ASR produces `the Mr. Kosky was saying` and `Mr. Horse Keeper` in contexts
   where the referent could be either the assistant superintendent or the board member. (The
   other 2/1/23 transcript renders the same sentence at `<340>` as `the Mr. was saying`, with no
   name at all.) Do not disambiguate
   these from the audio alone.

7. **Chair conflict at the 9/30/2024 joint meeting.** 16046 `<26>` renders a self-introduction as
   `Arlene Hawkins I'm on the curriculum subcommittee. I'm on the policy subcommittee, and I'm the
   chair of the school board.` Every other source in that period has Heather Whitney chairing
   Claremont and Hawkins chairing SAU 6 (and Whitney is the one running that meeting). Most likely
   the ASR dropped "SAU 6", but flagged rather than corrected.

8. **Names heard exactly once and never resolved:** `Mr. Beaupre` (12/18/24 finance `<577>`),
   `Mr. Braxton` (10/16/24 `<855>`), `Mr. Noda` (5/3/23 `<450>`), `Mr. Zenga` (6/21/23 `<362>`,
   7/19/23 `<701>`), `Mr. Monte` (7/19/23 `<95>`), `Jan O'Neil` the tech-center career counselor
   (12/18/24 `<261>`, also `Jennifer. I'm not sure for the last name` 10/2/24 `<288>`),
   `Miss Skinner` the food-service manager (6/21/23 `<187>`–`<297>` — distinct from `Deborah
   Skinner, art Stevens high school` in the 8/16/23 nomination list), `Miss Chastity` /
   `Miss Chasin` / `Miss Chastain` the Youth & Government advisor, `Allison Saint Aubin`
   (7/19/23 `<408>`), `Mark Manganiello` at the state DOE (8/16/23 `<22>`), and the 2/8/2023
   moderator, who is never named in that transcript.

9. **Diarized labels are not stable across meetings and are frequently wrong within a meeting.**
   Label counts run from 7 to 35 for bodies of 7–13 speakers; the same voice regularly carries
   several labels and a single label regularly carries several voices (e.g. 12/1/23, 13 labels for
   a 1,774-segment meeting, with `Speaker 1` covering both the chair and the presenting director).
   Use the labels as a weak grouping hint only.
